def fienup_target(a, target_func, n_iters...):
   Normalize p.
   M_fft=1<<(2n-1).bit_length()
   for it:
      P=rfft(p,M_fft); amp=|P|; phase=angle(P)
      amp_safe=max(amp, tiny)
      target = target_func(frequencies normalized)
      amp_new = amp_safe * (target/amp_safe)**alpha? We want move toward target. If target absolute scale, need normalization because Fienup amplitude scale determines sum? We can set amp_new = amp * (target/amp)**α. target median normalized so relative.
      P_new=amp_new*exp(i phase); p_new=irfft(P_new)[:n]; p_new=max(p_new, floor); normalize.

#### Mirror descent LSE implementation
Important for speed: Many iterations; each uses conv_self p (FFT length n1) and grad convolution w/p (FFT length n2). That's 2 rfft/irfft maybe 4 transforms. For n=7k, okay. 10000 iterations maybe. We can optimize by reusing c, weights? Need each iteration new p so new c.

Mirror update with backtracking LSE:
Pseudo:
def mirror_lse(p, beta_list, max_iter_total, min_time, start_eta=1.0, max_inner_line=8):
    p = sanitize(p)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_sc=score(p); worst? 
    for beta in beta_list:
        it=0; eta=start_eta
        for it in range(max_iter_beta):
            c=conv_self(p); Lval=lse_value(c,beta)
            if Lval < best_L maybe update best? But score exact not necessarily.
            w, _ = softmax_weights(c,beta)
            g=grad_weight(p,w)
            g = g - g.mean()
            # scale gradient
            gscale = np.linalg.norm(g)/np.sqrt(len(g)) + 1e-12
            g = g/gscale
            # line search
            accepted=False
            trial_eta=eta
            for _ in range(long attempts):
                u = -trial_eta * g
                # clip u to [-10,10] to avoid underflow
                u = np.clip(u, -10, 10)
                p_try = p * np.exp(u)
                S=p_try.sum()
                if not finite or S<1e-12:
                   trial_eta*=0.5; continue
                p_try/=S
                c_try=conv_self(p_try)
                L_try=lse_value(c_try,beta)
                if L_try <= Lval + 1e-15 or attempts_long:
                    accept
                else: trial_eta*=0.5
            if accept:
                 p=p_try; eta=min(eta*1.05, max_eta?)? Maybe keep.
                 compute exact score every 50 or if Lval close; if sc<best_sc update.
            else break
            if time exceed: break
        # after beta, maybe exact descent
    return best_p,best_sc

But LSE value decreases, exact max may increase at high beta? We track exact.

Pseudo mirror_lse:
```
def mirror_lse(p, ks=(1,3,10,30,100), max_iter=2000, time_end=None, eta0=0.1, eta_max=1.0):
    n=len(p); p=sanitize(p); best=copy; best_score=score
    for k in ks:
        beta = k*n
        eta=eta0
        stalled=0
        for it in range(max_iter):
            c=conv_self(p)
            L=lse_value(c,beta)
            # exact score check
            sc=2*n*c.max()
            if sc<best_score: update
            w=weights(c,beta)
            g=grad_weight(p,w)
            g -= g.mean()
            rms=np.sqrt(np.mean(g*g)); if rms<1e-15 break
            g /= rms
            best_ratio=...; best_ptry=None; best_L=L
            trial=eta
            accepted=False
            for line in range(8):
                u = -trial*g
                u = np.clip(u, -50, 50)
                p_try=p*np.exp(u)
                S=p_try.sum(); if S<1e-12: break
                p_try /= S
                c_try=conv_self(p_try)
                L_try=lse_value(c_try,beta)
                if L_try < best_L:
                    best_L=L_try; best_ptry=p_try; best_sc_try=2*n*c_try.max()
                if L_try <= L + 1e-14:
                    accepted=True; p=p_try; L=L_try; break
                trial *= 0.5
            if not accepted:
                # accept best if improved, else break
                if best_ptry is not None and best_L < L - 1e-15:
                    p=best_ptry; L=best_L
                else:
                    eta *=0.5
                    if eta<1e-4: break
                continue
            # if exact tidak improves? OK
            eta = min(eta_max, eta*1.05)
            if L decrease tiny: stalled++ else stalled=0
            if stalled>100: break
            if time_end and time>time_end: break
    return best, best_score
```
Potential issue: LSE value at different β cannot compare; but within β descent.

#### Direct peak reduction via quadratic solves
A powerful method: For active peaks, we can solve for step direction d that reduces active peaks and has zero sum by least squares. This is like projected gradient but stricter.
Given weights w (uniform active or exponential), the linearized change ∆c ≈ g = G d (where G matrix maps d to derivative). We want ∆c_k ≈ -c_k + M for active. This is underdetermined. A least-squares solution minimizing λ||d||² + sum w_k (G d + deficit_k)^2 gives d = (G^T W G + λI)^{-1} ( -G^T W deficit - ...). But G^T W G is convolution-like huge. We can apply to vector via FFT. Could use conjugate gradient for d? Too complex.

Simpler: Choose d = -A_w p as gradient, but project d to sum zero and perhaps negative parts? Mirror does.

def spectral_smooth_c(p, Ws=(4,8,16,32,64), n_iters=200):
   n=len(p); M_fft=1<<(2*n-1).bit_length()
   for W in Ws:
       pcur=sanitize(p)
       best=...
       for it in range(n_iters):
           c=conv_self(pcur)
           C=rfft(c, M_fft) # length M_fft//2+1? Actually c length 2n-1 zero-padded to M_fft.
           # build Fejer kernel length L=2n-1 with wrap distance <=W: h=1 - (dist/W)
           h=np.zeros(M_fft); dist = minimum(k, M_fft-k) but for linear? We want cyclic convolution length M_fft? The relation c length M_fft zero padded; using cyclic conv length M_fft. Fejer kernel on length M_fft.
           # DFT of h? Can build h and rfft.
           C_t = C * H
           c_t = irfft(C_t, M_fft)[:2n-1]
           c_t = np.maximum(c_t,0); scale sum to1
           # Now set P amplitude = sqrt(fft(c_t))
           T = rfft(c_t, M_fft)
           P = rfft(pcur, M_fft)
           phase = exp(i angle(P))
           P_new = sqrt(max(real(T),0))*phase

           p_new = np.maximum(p_new,1e-15); normalize
           sc=score(p_new); update

### Structured one-sided candidates

```
def base_candidates(n, seed):
  rs=[...]
  rng=default_rng(seed)
  x=np.arange(n)
  for alpha in [0.5,0.45,0.55,0.6]: rs.append(sanitize((x+1)**(-alpha)))
  reversed: rs.append(sanitize(rs[0][::-1]))
  # mixed: left + eps right
  left = ...; right=left[::-1]
  for eps in [0.0,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0]: rs.append(sanitize(left + eps*right))
  # central binomial recurrence
  c=np.empty(n); c[0]=1; for i in range(n-1): c[i+1]=c[i]*(2*i+1)/(2*i+2); rs.append(sanitize(c)); symmetric mix
  # edge peak: 1/sqrt(i+1) * (1 + 0.3*cos(π i/n)) maybe
  # random smooth: p=0.5+0.5cos? + noise
```

### Fienup shapes
Implement target shapes function:
```
def target_amplitude(kind, t, gamma):
   if kind=='flat': return np.ones_like(t)
   if kind=='peaked': return 1.0/(1.0 + gamma*t**2)
   if kind=='tri': return np.maximum(0.0, 1.0 - gamma*t)
   if kind=='exp': return np.exp(-gamma*t)
   if kind=='super': return (1.0 + gamma*(1.0-t))
   ...
```
In fienup:
```
def fienup_shape(p, kind, gamma, alpha, n_iters, time_end):
  p=sanitize; M=...
  t = np.r_[0, np.arange(1, len(rfftfreq?))]/(n_fft//2) maybe normalized 0..1.
  target = target_amplitude(kind,t,gamma); target /= median(target)
  for it:
    A=rfft(p,M_fft); amp=abs(A); phase=angle(A); scale=median(amp); target_scaled=target*scale
    amp_new = amp_safe * (target_scaled/amp_safe)**alpha
    # Maybe not alter DC? DC determines sum; but normalization after. Keep phase.
    A_new=amp_new*np.exp(1j*phase)
    p_new=irfft(A_new,M_fft)[:n]
    p_new=np.clip(p_new,0,None); normalize
    sc=score; track
```
Parameter alpha from 0.1 to 0.9 maybe schedule. We can run each shape for max iterations until time.

### frequency_descent
Implement carefully with rfft length M_fft. Let L=2n-1; M_fft power of two >=L.
```
def freq_descent(p, n_iters, frac=0.2, beta=None, step0=0.05, time_end=None):
  p=sanitize; best
  M=1<<(2*n-1).bit_length()
  t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
  for it:
    P=rfft(p,M)
    C=P*P.real? Actually A*A complex; c=irfft(A*A,M)[:L]
    w from c: use softmax if beta not None, or active quantile.
    W=rfft(w, M)
    Wnorm = W / np.linalg.norm(W?) maybe W/ (w.sum()*M) to keep DC ~1.
    step = step0/(1+it/100)
    factor = 1 - step*Wnorm
    # Prevent negative real? P complex; factor complex.
    P2 = P*factor
    p2=irfft(P2,M)[:n]
    # Maybe take positive part with soft-threshold: p2 = np.maximum(p2,0)
    # For smoother, p2 = p * sigmoid? Use clip.
    p2 = np.maximum(p2, 1e-15)
    p2/=p2.sum()
    update score
  return best
```

But factor could have magnitude >1 for some frequency if W negative real, increasing P and making c peaks? Backtracking? We can try line search: compute p2 score; if worse, reduce step. Simpler: keep best, try step in [0.05,0.02,...]. We'll implement a small inner line search:

Gradient function `grad_from_weights(p,w)` with w length 2n-1. Implementation:
```
def grad_from_weights(p,w):
  n=len(p)
  L=2*n-1
  p_rev=p[::-1]
  m=1 << (L+n-1).bit_length()  # L+n-1=3n-2
  Wr=np.fft.rfft(w,m)
  Pr=np.fft.rfft(p_rev,m)
  cross=np.fft.irfft(Wr*Pr,m)[n-1:2*n-1]
  g=2.0*cross
  diag_indices=np.arange(0,L,2)
  # i where 2i<L: i=0..(L-1)/2 = n-1
  diag=w[0:L:2] * p
  g -= diag
  return g
```

### Parameter tuning
We need choose hyperparameters likely. Let's detail each function.

#### `sanitize(a, floor=1e-20)`
```
def sanitize(a, floor=1e-20):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    S=a.sum()
    if S < 1e-12:
       return np.ones_like(a)/len(a)
    a=np.maximum(a, floor)
    S=a.sum()
    if S <1e-12: return ones
    return a/S
```
But adding floor to all n can add n*1e-20 negligible. If len huge 1e5 no issue.

#### `conv_self(a)`
```
def conv_self(a):
   n=len(a)
   L=2*n-1
   m=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
   A=np.fft.rfft(a.astype(float), m)
   c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, m)[:L]
   # remove tiny negative
   c[c < 0] = 0.0? If small negative, maybe set 0. But if large negative impossible. Use np.maximum(c,0)
   return c
```
Use `A * A` not conjugate? For real a, rfft A complex; convolution uses A*A (not conjugate) because DFT of a times itself (not autocorrelation) (autocorrelation would A*conj(A)). Since p real, P(ω)^2, not |P|². Important! Wait earlier I thought |A|². For linear convolution of a with itself, DFT = A(ω)^2, not |A(ω)|². Yes! We made a major mistake. The objective is coefficients of P(x)^2, not power spectrum. Frequency-domain relation is complex square, not magnitude squared. This changes everything! c_k are coefficients of P^2. The DFT of c is P(ω)^2, complex, not nonnegative. That's why continuous inequality different. The c-domain PSD idea invalid: c not autocorrelation, can have negative Fourier transform. This is crucial.

Our previous Fienup amplitude shaping was shaping amplitude of A, not phase? But convolution uses square, phase matters quadratically. c are not nonnegative in Fourier. The target flat c corresponds P(ω)^2 delta, P(ω) can be sqrt(delta) with phase? P real coefficients? Hmm.

Need adjust algorithms:
- conv_self should use `A * A`, not `A * A.conj()`. We had correct in previous code: fast_conv uses A*A. Good.
- But gradient formula? c_k = ∑ p_i p_{k-i} is polynomial square. DFT is P². The derivative c_k wrt p_i still 2 p_{k-i}. The weighted sum ∑ w_k c_k gradient is 2 cross as derived (convolution of weights with p, no Fourier relation simple except P²). - Frequency-domain c-domain PSD smoothing invalid because c not autocorrelation. We cannot set |P|² from c; c's DFT is P² (complex). If we want target c_t, we need square root P = sqrt(DFT(c_t)) complex, not magnitude. That may be possible! c_t need not be PSD; its DFT can be complex. We can take complex square root with phase chosen. This is spectral factorization for general polynomials, not autocorrelation. That is more powerful: Given target coefficient c_t, compute F = FFT(c_t). Need P = sqrt(F) (one of two roots) such that IFFT(P) has nonnegative support. We can choose principal branch and phase. Indeed the square root of flat coefficient polynomial (1+...+x^{2n-2}) has coefficients with signs, as recurrence; finite root may have complex/negative. But we can use phase optimization.

This opens new algorithm: Directly optimize target convolution c (length 2n-1) to be low max, then spectral factorize to P by complex sqrt of its DFT, inverse, project p nonnegative. This is like polynomial square root, not autocorrelation. Previous Fienup amplitude shaping phase fixed was poor; we can update phase of P via inverse c target.

We can iterate:
1. Start p (or target c).

4. Compute E=FFT(c_t); P_new = sqrt(E) (complex, with branch selection); p_new = IFFT(P_new)[:n]; project nonnegative and sum.
This is like generalized Fienup with target in c-domain and spectral square root. Since we aren't constrained to |P| nonnegative? P can be complex, but p should real. def waterfill_c(c, reduction=0.0):
   # c nonnegative sum~1
   # Compute level water from original c:
   d=np.sort(c)[::-1]
   L=len(c)
   cum=np.concatenate(([0], np.cumsum(d)))
   # For k top set to λ = (1 - sum_rest)/k. Need λ <= d[k-1] and λ < d[0]
   # find largest k? Actually as k increases λ may increase? We want λ < max; choose first k where λ <= d[k-1] (top k threshold). The resulting max=max(λ, d[k]) (next). ```

Implementation:
```
def waterfill_c(c, reduction_frac=0.0):
   c=c.copy(); S=c.sum(); c/=S
   L=len(c)
   d=np.sort(c)[::-1]
   cum=np.concatenate(([0.0],np.cumsum(d)))
   level=None; top_k=0
   for k in range(1,L+1):
       lam=(1.0 - cum[k-1])/k
       if lam <= d[k-1] + 1e-14:
           level=lam; top_k=k; break
   if level is None: level=1.0/L; top_k=L
   # if we want enforce lower, level = min(level, c.max()*(1-reduction_frac))? But sum then. We can adjust after.
   # pick top top_k by argpartition
   if top_k < L:
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]
   else: idx=np.arange(L)
   c_t=c.copy()
   c_t[idx]=level
   # due floating sum maybe fix: c_t /= c_t.sum()
   return c_t
```
This always makes max = max(level, next below) ≤ original max; likely close to flat. Distance: For c from p, subtract? It might jump too much, root may poor. We can blend: `c_t = (1-frac)*c + frac*waterfill(c)`, with frac increasing.

We can also do `clip_water`: reduce top fraction only to quantile and scale sum: simpler and mild.
```
def mild_clip_c(c, top_frac):
   d=np.quantile(c,1-top_frac)
   c_t=np.minimum(c,d)
   # preserve sum by scaling up
   c_t *= 1.0/c_t.sum()
   return c_t
```
Max after scaling = d*scale. scale=1/(1 - loss). If top_frac small, slight. We'll use top_frac 0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2 with fracs.

Also can use `flat_target`: c = ones(L)/L; blend with current: `c_t = (1-mix)*c + mix*flat` (sum1 if c sum1). Flat target degree? Root of flat may have complex p? But blending could approach.

Then sqrt phase selection:
```
def root_step(p, c_t, M_fft):
   F = np.fft.rfft(c_t, M_fft)
   S = np.sqrt(F)  # complex principal. For Nyquist, imag may exist; keep complex? irfft uses only real for Nyquist. Could set last = S[-1].real.
   P = np.fft.rfft(p, M_fft)
   # branch align with P: for each m, if real(P*m S) <0: S=-S
   # But P may be zero; then keep principal.
   align_re = np.real(P * np.conj(S))
   S[align_re < 0] *= -1
   # Nyquist maybe choose sign to make real part positive? Not critical.
   return np.fft.irfft(S, M_fft)[:n]
```

def root_fienup(p, c_mode='water', top_frac=0.1, mix=0.5, alpha=0.5, n_iters=200, time_end=None):
  p=sanitize; best
  n=len(p); L=2*n-1; M=1<<(L-1).bit_length()
  for it in range(n_iters):
    c=conv_self(p); c_sum=c.sum(); if <... break
    if c_mode=='water':
       cw=waterfill_c(c)
    elif 'clip': cw=clip_c(c,top_frac)
    elif 'flat': cw=np.ones(L)/L
    else: cw=c
    c_t = (1-mix)*c + mix*cw
    c_t=np.maximum(c_t,0); c_t/=c_t.sum()
    F=np.fft.rfft(c_t, M)
    S=np.sqrt(F)
    # for Nyquist (last), imag part? irfft uses real, set S[-1]=S[-1].real maybe sign branch
    P=np.fft.rfft(p, M)
    re=P*np.conj(S)
    S[re.real <0] *= -1
    # optional: for very small |S|, set to 0 to avoid noise? If |S|<1e-14: S=0
    raw=np.fft.irfft(S, M)[:n]
    raw=np.maximum(raw,0)
    p_try=(1-alpha)*p + alpha*(raw/raw.sum() if raw.sum()>... else p)
    p_try=sanitize(p_try)
    sc=score; update
    if time... break
  return best
```

def waterfill_c(c):
    c=np.asarray(c,float).copy()
    S=c.sum()
    if S<=0: return c
    c/=S
    d=np.sort(c)[::-1]
    cum=np.empty(len(d)+1); cum[0]=0; cum[1:]=np.cumsum(d)
    # Find k >=1 such that lam=(1-cum[k-1])/k <= d[k-1]
    # Use vectorized maybe.
    k=np.arange(1,len(d)+1)
    lam=(1.0 - cum[:-1]) / k
    # choose largest k? The waterfill active set is largest k satisfying lam >= d[k]? Standard: choose k where lam between d[k-1] and d[k]. If scan from k=1, first where lam <= d[k-1] is top? def clip_scale_c(c, red, top_frac?):
   oldmax=c.max()
   # two options: clip all above oldmax*(1-red)
   tau=oldmax*(1-red)
   c2=np.minimum(c,tau)
   S=c2.sum()
   if S<=0: tau=oldmax*0.5...
   c2/=S
```

Let's implement root_phase with target builder 'flat_blend', 'clip', 'smooth'.
**Flat blend** is very simple, no complex waterfill. It may reduce objective. Let's use as primary.

Root function:
```
def root_flat_blending(p, alphas=(0.05,0.1,...), n_iters_each=200, newton_steps=2, proj_blend=0.5, time_end=None):
   p=sanitize
   L=2n-1; M=...
   flat = np.ones(L)/L
   for alpha in alphas:
      for it:
         c=conv_self(p)
         c_t=(1-alpha)*c + alpha*flat
         c_t=np.maximum(c_t,0); c_t/=c_t.sum()
         F=fft(c_t,M)
         P=rfft(p,M)
         for ns in range(newton_steps):
             absP=np.abs(P)
             eps=1e-12*max(1, np.max(np.abs(F))**0.5)
             P_safe=P.copy()
             mask=absP>eps
             P_safe[mask]=P[mask]
             P_safe[~mask]=np.sqrt(F[~mask]) + 0j
             # if F zero and P zero, zero
             P_new=0.5*(P_safe + F/P_safe)
             # For entries where abs(F)<epsF: set P_new=0?
             P=P_new
             P[-1]=P[-1].real
         raw=irfft(P,M)[:n]
         pos=np.maximum(raw,0)
         Spos=pos.sum()
         if Spos<1e-12: p_try=p; else p_try=(1-proj_blend)*p + proj_blend*(pos/Spos)
         p=sanitize(p_try)
         update best
```
But careful: `P = rfft(p,M)` each outer; after Newton P corresponds to target not necessarily p; we invert and project, then next loop recompute P from projected p, losing spectral update? That's split-step. Good. Alternatively keep P updated until inverse? But time projection needs p. We can set P to rfft(sanitized p_try) next iteration.

Newton root of F=(target c) each iteration starting from current P approximates sqrt target. If target changes slowly, continuity. Good.

Could we do multiple splits: For each alpha, do `raw` becomes closer to a solution; update score. This might be powerful.

Let's test concept on uniform p: c triangular, flat blend target reduces middle. Newton P from target. It may produce p with one-sided singular? Maybe.

Potential issue: Fourier length M power of two; flat length L has zeros? F has complex values. Newton okay.

### Frequency DFT exact root for current c
If c_t=c, Newton should leave P unchanged (if P nonzero). But due zero padding M>L, c length L zero-padded. P=FFT(p zero padded). F=FFT(c)=P²? Yes. Newton converges. raw=irfft(P): support first n? ### Use of branch signs with Newton

Let's plan proposed program in detail:

Top-level docstring: "Polish step-function coefficients using polynomial square-root target iteration, mirror-descent on smoothed peak, and spectral phase search."

Imports: numpy as np, time. Maybe no scipy.

Functions:
1. `sanitize_sequence(a, floor=1e-20)`
2. `fast_self_conv(a)`

#### internal_score
```
def internal_score(a):
    n=len(a)
    S=a.sum()
    if S<1e-12: return float('inf')
    c=fast_self_conv(a)
    return float(2.0*n*float(np.max(c))/(S*S))
```
For p sum1, could use 2n max.

#### lse
```
def lse_from_c(c,beta):
    m=float(np.max(c))
    z=beta*(c-m)
    zmax=float(np.max(z))  # 0 maybe
    z=z-zmax
    s=float(np.sum(np.exp(z)))
    if s <=0: return m
    return m + zmax/beta + float(np.log(s))/beta
```
If beta=0? not.

#### softmax weights
```
def softmax_weights(c,beta):
    m=max; z=beta*(c-m); z-=max; w=exp(z); s=sum; if s<... uniform top? return w/s
```

#### gradient
```
def gradient_from_weights(p,w):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    p_rev=p[::-1]
    conv_len=L+n-1
    m=1 << (conv_len-1).bit_length()
    Wr=np.fft.rfft(w,m)
    Pr=np.fft.rfft(p_rev,m)
    cross=np.fft.irfft(Wr*Pr,m)[n-1:2*n-1]
    return 2.0*cross
```
Test derivative with n=1: L=1, conv_len=1, m=1, cross=w0p0, grad=2w0p0 correct.

#### mirror_smooth_descent
Let's design schedule inside one function to simplify.
```
def mirror_smooth_descent(p, max_iter=2000, eta0=0.05, eta_max=0.5, n_momentum=0.9, time_end=None, ks=(1,3,10,30)):
    p=sanitize; best=p; best_sc=inf
    n=len(p)
    g_buf=np.zeros(n); mom=0.0
    for k in ks:
        beta=max(1.0, k*n)
        eta=eta0
        stalled=0
        for it in range(max_iter):
           c=fast_self_conv(p)
           sc=2*n*c.max()
           if sc<best_sc: best=...
           w=softmax_weights(c,beta)
           g=gradient_from_weights(p,w)
           g -= np.mean(g)
           # momentum in gradient: g_buf = mom*g_buf + (1-mom)*g
           g_buf = n_momentum*g_buf + (1-n_momentum)*g
           rms=np.sqrt(np.mean(g_buf*g_buf)); if rms<1e-15 break
           g_use=g_buf/rms
           # line search on LSE
           L0=lse_from_c(c,beta)
           trial=eta; accepted=False; best_trial=None; best_L=L0; best_p=None; best_sc_t=sc
           for ls in range(6):
              u=-trial*g_use
              u=np.clip(u,-20,20)
              p_try=p*np.exp(u)
              S=p_try.sum(); if bad break
              p_try/=S
              c_try=fast_self_conv(p_try)
              L_try=lse_from_c(c_try,beta)
              sc_try=2*n*c_try.max()
              if L_try<best_L:
                 best_L=L_try; best_p=p_try; best_sc_t=sc_try
              if L_try <= L0 + 1e-15:
                 accepted=True; p=p_try; L=L_try; if sc_try<best_sc update; break
              trial*=0.5
          if not accepted:
             if best_p is not None and best_L < L0 - 1e-15:
                p=best_p; L=best_L; if best_sc_t<best_sc update
             # decay eta
             eta *=0.7
             if eta<1e-4: break
             continue
          eta = min(eta_max, eta*1.05)
          # If L decreased very little after some, break?
          if time_end and time.monotonic()>time_end: break
    return best, best_sc
```
Momentum with different beta? Keep g_buf across beta may carry; maybe reset at each beta. We'll reset per k.
Time check inside loops.
Line search 6 conv per iter may be heavy. Could reduce to 3. We can make `line_trials=4`.

Better: Instead of line search, use fixed eta with adaptive damping: try one step; if L increases, halve. Use `line_trials=4`. Good.

For exact descent:
```
def mirror_exact_descent(p, max_iter=2000, eta0=0.05, active_frac=0.02, time_end=None):
  p=sanitize; best...
  eta=eta0; g_buf=zeros; mom=0.8
  for it:
    c=conv; M=c.max(); sc=2nM; update
    q=np.quantile(c, 1-active_frac)
    tol=max(1e-15, 1e-6*M)
    active=(c>=max(q, M-tol))
    if active.sum()<5: active = topk 50?
    w=active/active.sum()
    g=grad; project; momentum; line search exact M
  return best
```

#### root_square_target
```
def root_square_target(p, mode='flat', strength=0.2, top_frac=0.1, proj_blend=0.5, newton_steps=2, n_iters=200, time_end=None):
    p=sanitize; best...
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1; M=1<<(L-1).bit_length()
    flat = np.ones(L)/L
    for it in range(n_iters):
       c=fast_self_conv(p)
       if mode=='flat':
           c_t = (1-strength)*c + strength*flat
       elif mode=='clip':
           # find tau = max(c)* (1-strength) maybe; c2=np.minimum(c,tau); c_t=(1-strength? no) maybe blend with current
           tau=max(c.max()*(1-strength), c.mean())
           c2=np.minimum(c,tau); c2/=c2.sum(); c_t=(1-strength)*c + strength*c2
       elif mode=='smooth':
           # moving average target weights = [window]
           kernel_len=max(3, int(strength*10)+1)? not
       c_t=np.maximum(c_t,0); c_t/=c_t.sum()
       F=np.fft.rfft(c_t, M)
       P=np.fft.rfft(p, M)
       # Newton steps
       maxF=np.max(np.abs(F)).real? abs complex
       eps=1e-12 * max(1.0, np.sqrt(maxF))
       for _ in range(newton_steps):
           absP=np.abs(P)
           P_safe=P.copy()
           small = absP < eps
           if small.any():
               # principal sqrt for small
               P_safe[small] = np.sqrt(F[small])
           # if F is tiny, set P to zero? 
           zeroF = np.abs(F) < eps*eps
           P_new = 0.5*(P_safe + np.zeros_like(F))
           # avoid divide by zero already
           with np.errstate? Use P_safe where |F| > tiny.
           valid = np.abs(F) > eps
           P_new[valid] = 0.5*(P_safe[valid] + F[valid]/P_safe[valid])
           P_new[~valid] = P_safe[~valid] * 0.5? Maybe if F zero, root zero.
           P = P_new
           P[-1] = P[-1].real
       raw=np.fft.irfft(P, M)[:n]
       pos=np.maximum(raw,0.0)
       Spos=pos.sum()
       if Spos < 1e-12:
           continue
       p_try = (1-proj_blend)*p + proj_blend*(pos/Spos)
       p=sanitize(p_try)
       sc=internal_score(p)
       update
       if time_end and time>end: break
    return best, sc
```

   # also clip mode

Let's implement `top_clip_target` too:
```
def top_clip_target(c, frac, red):
   c=c.copy(); oldmax=max(c)
   if frac <=0: return c
   d=np.quantile(c,1-frac)
   # clip top to d, then reduce d by red
   tau=max(d*(1-red), c.mean())
   c2=np.minimum(c,tau)
   S=c2.sum(); if S>0 c2/=S
   return c2
```
Then blend `c_t=(1-strength)*c + strength*c2`. Grid strength 0.1..0.5, frac 0.05,0.1,0.2.

def resample_mass(a, n_new):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); old=len(a)
    if n_new==old: return a
    if n_new < old:
       # average blocks by cumulative indices: Use np.array_split? Simpler: indices=np.linspace(0,old,n_new+1); q=np.array([a[int(i):int(j)].sum() for...]) but loops O(n). Loop with Python for n_new <=12000 okay but better vectorized.
       idx_floor=np.floor(np.linspace(0,old,n_new+1)).astype(int)
       q=np.diff? Need sum over intervals: use np.take? Could use cumulative sum: cum=np.cumsum(a); q=cum[idx_floor[1:]-1] - cum[idx_floor[:-1]-1] careful zeros.
    else:
       # assign each new to old bin floor(old*i/n_new) and divide by count
       old_idx=np.floor(np.arange(n_new)*old/n_new).astype(int)
       counts=np.bincount(old_idx, minlength=old)
       q=np.zeros(n_new); q[old_idx]=counts? Need distribute: For each old j, mass a[j]/counts[j] to its new positions. We can do np.add.at? Easier: counts; q = a[old_idx] / counts[old_idx]
    return sanitize(q)
```
For downsample, use cum method. If interval empty, q=0.
```
def resample_mass(a,n_new):
  old=len(a)
  if n_new==old: return sanitize(a)
  if n_new<old:
     node=np.linspace(0,old,n_new+1)
     left=np.floor(node).astype(int); right=np.ceil(node).astype(int)
     # To preserve exactly, use cumulative: start=left, end=right; but overlaps due rounding. Better indices = np.round(np.linspace(0,old,n_new+1)).astype(int)
     idx=np.linspace(0,old,n_new+1).round().astype(int)
     idx[0]=0; idx[-1]=old
     cum=np.concatenate(([0],np.cumsum(a)))
     q=cum[idx[1:]]-cum[idx[:-1]]
  else:
     old_idx=(np.arange(n_new)*old/n_new).astype(int)
     counts=np.bincount(old_idx,minlength=old)
     counts[counts==0]=1
     q=a[old_idx]/counts[old_idx]
  return sanitize(q)
```
This preserves sum.

### Candidate generation details
```
def structured_candidates(n, rng, parent=None):
  cand=[]
  # uniform
  cand.append(np.ones(n)/n)
  # one-sided powers
  x=np.arange(n)+1.0
  for a in [0.5,0.45,0.55]: cand.append(sanitize(x**(-a)))
  # reversed
  left=x**(-0.5); cand.append(sanitize(left)); cand.append(sanitize(left[::-1]))
  for eps in [0.1,0.5,1.0]: cand.append(sanitize(left + eps*left[::-1]))
  # central binomial recurrence
  cb=np.empty(n); cb[0]=1; for i... cand.append(sanitize(cb)); cand.append(sanitize(cb[::-1])); cand.append(sanitize(cb+cb[::-1]))
  # cosine taper one-sided: x^-0.5*(0.7+0.3*cos(pi*i/n))
  # random smooth if parent
  if parent is not None:
     cand.append(sanitize(parent if len==n else resample_mass(parent,n)))
     cand.append(sanitize(parent + 0.05*randnormal))
  return cand
```
But building n=12000 for many candidates with recurrence loop fine.

At each optimization, choose current best; if a weak candidate after root improves, use. Simpler: run all candidates through a short root (e.g., 30 iterations at alpha=0.1) to see if they improve, then track. But time.

P_safe = P.copy()

P_new[~np.isfinite(P_new)] = P[~]

### More analysis of root target and objective
The root target flat suggests p toward polynomial square root of flat length. For normalized flat c=1/L, target C(x)=(1+...+x^{2n-2})/L. A formal square root at x=0 has coefficients p_i = (1/√L) * central binomial? Actually sqrt flat = sqrt(1+...+x^{2n-2})/√L = (1/√L)*(1-x^{2n-1})^{1/2}(1-x)^{-1/2}. Coefficients for i<2n-1 are central binomial (positive), for larger negative; truncated degree n-1 positive but square not exactly flat; missing high terms cause middle/tail deviations. The root DFT Newton with finite DFT may choose a different root (with both signs) that better matches within length. This could yield improved p beyond one-sided. It might converge to known optimum. Good.

We can also target not flat but **optimal c lower bound**: Since n factor objective target σ, max allowed M=σ/(2n). We can set target c with all coefficients ≤ some level and sum1, e.g. c_t = min(c, level)/level? But flat already.

### Could we directly construct p from c target by solving polynomial square root in time via recurrence? For a target c (degree 2n-2), if it is exactly a square of degree n-1, recurrence determines p from p0 and c. We can choose p0 to make p nonnegative. For near square, we can solve least squares for p to match c: minimize ||P² - c_t||². This is nonlinear but gradient =? Could use Newton (our DFT root is global in frequency but not enforcing time support). Time-domain Newton for p: P² residual r, Jacobian 2P? Not.
DFT Newton with time projection is good.

### Performance estimation
Root iteration each:
- conv_self p: rfft+irfft length M~16k => 2 transforms.
- rfft c_t: 1 transform.
- rfft p: maybe combine with conv? Conv already computed A=rfft(p,M); we can reuse A for Newton instead of rfft p again. We can modify `root_square_target` to compute A in conv_self and return A? Function returns c but not A. To optimize, implement inside root: compute `A = np.fft.rfft(p,M); c=irfft(A*A,M)[:L]`. Then use A as P. Good. This saves one transform. Similarly in mirror, conv_self computes A but discards; for gradient we need p. Fine.

Root iteration transforms: A p (rfft), c=irfft(A²), F=rfft(c_t), Newton no transforms, raw=irfft(P). Total 3 FFT/irfft + one rfft c_t? Actually F rfft is another. So 4 transforms. Mirror gradient: conv (rfft+irfft), gradient conv (two rfft and irfft) =4 transforms per iter + line search more. Root cheap.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    t_start=time.monotonic(); total=float(budget_s); time_avail=total-60; deadline=t_start+time_avail
    # parent
    parent=None
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float).ravel()
       if arr.size>=2:
          arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
          if arr.sum()>1e-8: parent=sanitize(arr)
    except Exception: pass
    MAX_LEN=16384
    if parent is not None:
        if len(parent)>MAX_LEN: parent=resample_mass(parent, MAX_LEN)
        n_main=len(parent)
    else:
        n_main=8192
    best=parent if parent is not None else np.ones(n_main)/n_main
    best_score=internal_score(best)
    print
    # Generate candidates
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    candidates=[]
    if parent is not None:
       candidates.append(parent)
    candidates += structured_candidates(n_main, rng, parent)
    # evaluate and store top
    cand_scores=[]
    for c in candidates:
       c=sanitize(c)
       if c.size != n_main: continue
       sc=internal_score(c)
       if sc < best_score: best=c; best_score=sc; print
       cand_scores.append((sc,c))
    cand_scores.sort(key=lambda x:x[0]) # lambda disallowed? It says no lambda functions. Use `cand_scores.sort()` because tuple first score. Good.
    # top list up to 5
    top_candidates = [c for sc,c in cand_scores[:5]]
    # Phase 1: root on best with schedule
    t1 = t_start + 0.25*time_avail
    # root flat small to large
    for mode,alpha,pblend, stage_frac in [('flat',0.05,0.8,0.1), ...]:
       end = t_start + stage_frac*time_avail
       b,sc = root_square_target(best, mode, strength=alpha, proj_blend=pblend, n_iters=100000, time_end=end)
       if sc<best_score: best=b; best_score=sc
    # Phase 2: root on top few diverse if time
    t2 = t_start + 0.45*time_avail
    for c in top_candidates[1:4]:
       if time>t2: break
       b,sc = root_square_target(c, 'flat', strength=0.2, proj_blend=0.7, n_iters=500, time_end=t2)
       update
       # maybe smooth short
    # Phase 3: mirror smooth
    t3 = t_start + 0.75*time_avail
    b,sc = mirror_smooth_descent(best, max_iter=100000, time_end=t3, ks=(1,3,10,30,100))
    update
    # Phase 4: exact mirror
    t4 = t_start + 0.90*time_avail
    b,sc = mirror_exact_descent(best, max_iter=100000, time_end=t4, active_frac=0.02)
    update
    # Phase 5: final root strong + exact
    b,sc = root_square_target(best,'flat',strength=0.45,proj_blend=0.5,n_iters=2000,time_end=deadline)
    update
    b,sc = mirror_exact_descent(best, max_iter=100000, time_end=deadline, active_frac=0.01, eta0=0.02)
    update
    # maybe perturbation restart
    for noise in [0.01,0.02]:
       if time>deadline: break
       p=best*(1+0.01*rng.standard_normal(len(best)))
       p=sanitize(p)
       b,sc=mirror_exact_descent(p,max_iter=500,time_end=deadline,active_frac=0.02)
       update
    # Final length check
    if len(best)>MAX_LEN: best=resample_mass(best,MAX_LEN); best_score=internal_score(best)
    out...
```

### `structured_candidates` no closures
Implement with loops. `cb` recurrence for n=16384 fine.
```
def build_structured_candidates(n, rng, parent):
   cands=[]
   cands.append(np.ones(n)*0.5)  # sanitize later
   x=np.arange(n,dtype=float)+1.0
   for alpha in [0.45,0.5,0.55]: cands.append(x**(-alpha))
   left=x**(-0.5); right=left[::-1]
   cands.append(left); cands.append(right)
   for eps in [0.1,0.3,1.0]: cands.append(left + eps*right)
   # central binomial
   cb=np.empty(n); cb[0]=1.0
   for i in range(n-1): cb[i+1]=cb[i]*(2*i+1)/(2*i+2)
   cands.append(cb); cands.append(cb[::-1]); cands.append(cb+cb[::-1])
   # tapered
   i=np.arange(n)/max(1,n-1)
   cands.append(left*(0.7+0.3*np.cos(np.pi*i)))
   # random smooth
   base=np.ones(n)*0.5
   for _ in range(4): base +=0.1*np.cos(2*np.pi*rng.uniform(1,8)*i + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
   cands.append(np.maximum(base,0.05))
   if parent is not None:
       if len(parent)==n:
          cands.append(parent.copy())
       else:
          cands.append(resample_mass(parent,n))
       p=sanitize(parent if len(parent)==n else resample_mass(parent,n))
       for noise in [0.01,0.03]:
          cands.append(p*(1+noise*rng.standard_normal(n)))
   return cands
```
Uniform values all 0.5; sanitize normalizes.

### root mode 'clip'
Implement:
```
def clip_target(c, frac, red):
    c=c.copy()
    if frac <=0: return c
    q=np.quantile(c, 1.0 - frac)
    tau=max(q*(1.0 - red), np.mean(c))
    c2=np.minimum(c,tau)
    S=c2.sum()
    if S<=0: return c
    return c2/S
```
In root_square_target mode 'clip', `c_t=(1-strength)*c_used + strength*clip_target(c_used, top_frac, strength maybe)`; need pass `top_frac`. We'll add parameter.

def fienup_amplitude(p, target_kind='peaked', gamma=5.0, alpha=0.5, n_iters=500, time_end=None):
   p=sanitize; best...
   M=1<<(2n-1).bit_length()
   t=np.linspace(0.0,1.0, M//2+1)
   if target_kind=='flat': target=np.ones_like(t)
   elif 'peaked': target=1/(1+gamma*t**2)
   elif 'exp': target=np.exp(-gamma*t)
 else
   med=np.median(target); target/=med
   for it:
      A=rfft(p,M); amp=abs(A); phase=angle(A); scale=median(amp)
      target_scaled=target*scale

      p_try=irfft(A_new,M)[:n]; pos=clip; normalize; score update

def topk_weights(c, limit):
   if limit>=len(c): w=1/len(c)
   else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -limit)[-limit:]; w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[idx]=1/limit; return w
```

### Let's think more deeply to ensure objective and gradient correct.
For p normalized sum1. c_k = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1} p_i p_{k-i} with p_j=0 outside. Let w_k weights sum1. F=∑ w_k c_k. Derivative: ∂F/∂p_i=∑_k w_k (p_{k-i} + p_{k-i})? Wait earlier derivative for offdiag 2p. For i=1: cross=w1p0+w2p1; 2cross=2w1p0+2w2p1. Good. So gradient=2cross.
For active max w uniform over active sum1, subgradient valid.

LSE L = m + (1/β)log∑e^{β(c-m)}. dL/dc = (∑e^{...}/sum) = w. So gradient=2cross.

### Root method details and branch
Let's write robust Newton:
```
def spectral_sqrt_newton(P, F, steps):
    for _ in range(steps):
        absP = np.abs(P)
        # threshold based on F
        threshold = 1e-12 * (1.0 + np.sqrt(np.max(np.abs(F))))
        small = absP <= threshold
        P_safe = P.copy()
        if np.any(small):
            P_safe[small] = np.sqrt(F[small])
        # for very small F, root should maybe 0; avoid large division
        absF = np.abs(F)
        valid = absF > threshold*threshold
        P_new = P.copy()
        if np.any(valid):
            P_new[valid] = 0.5 * (P_safe[valid] + F[valid] / P_safe[valid])
        if np.any(~valid):
            # gradually zero small target roots
            P_new[~valid] = 0.5 * P_safe[~valid]
        P = P_new
    return P
```
If P_safe small replaced by sqrt(F), division okay unless F also zero; valid false. If F tiny but P large, valid false and P_new=0.5P, decays. Good.
Set `P[-1] = P[-1].real`.
Potential issue `np.sqrt(F[small])` for F with large negative real creates imaginary; okay.

Could also do **magnitude scaling**: After Newton, P may grow if target flat and current P not root; but DC fixed to1. Fine.

### Let's refine `root_square_target` code to be efficient and robust

```
def root_square_target(p, mode='flat', strength=0.2, top_frac=0.1, proj_blend=0.7,
                      n_iters=1000, time_end=None, newton_steps=2):
    p = sanitize(p)
    n = len(p)
    L = 2*n - 1
    M = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    flat = np.ones(L, dtype=float) / L
    best = p.copy(); best_sc = internal_score(p)
    p_cur = p.copy()
    for _ in range(n_iters):
        if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
            break
        A = np.fft.rfft(p_cur, M)
        c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, M)[:L]
        cmax = float(np.max(c))
        sc = 2.0 * n * cmax  # p sum 1
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc; best = p_cur.copy()
        c_for = c.copy(); c_for[c_for < 0.0] = 0.0
        ssum = c_for.sum()
        if ssum <= 0: break
        c_for /= ssum
        if mode == 'flat':
            c_t = (1.0 - strength) * c_for + strength * flat
        elif mode == 'clip':
            c2 = top_clip_target(c_for, top_frac, strength)
            c_t = (1.0 - strength) * c_for + strength * c2
        elif mode == 'smooth':
            # maybe smoothing by averaging with flat? same
            c_t = c_for
        c_t = np.maximum(c_t, 0.0)
        st = c_t.sum()
        if st <= 0: break
        c_t /= st
        F = np.fft.rfft(c_t, M)
        P = A.copy()
        maxF = float(np.max(np.abs(F)))
        eps = max(1e-14, 1e-12 * max(1.0, np.sqrt(maxF)))
        for _ns in range(newton_steps):
            absP = np.abs(P)
            P_safe = P.copy()
            small = absP <= eps
            if np.any(small):
                P_safe[small] = np.sqrt(F[small])
            valid = np.abs(F) > eps * eps
            P_new = P.copy()
            if np.any(valid):
                P_new[valid] = 0.5 * (P_safe[valid] + F[valid] / P_safe[valid])
            if np.any(~valid):
                P_new[~valid] = 0.5 * P_safe[~valid]
            P = P_new
            P[-1] = P[-1].real
        raw = np.fft.irfft(P, M)[:n]
        pos = np.maximum(raw, 0.0)
        sp = float(pos.sum())
        if sp < 1e-12:
            p_cur = p_cur  # no change?
            continue
        pos = pos / sp
        if proj_blend <= 0.0:
            p_try = pos
        elif proj_blend >= 1.0:
            p_try = pos
        else:
            p_try = (1.0 - proj_blend) * p_cur + proj_blend * pos
        p_try = sanitize(p_try)
        # Try both direct and blend? Maybe compute score direct if p_try worse
        sc_try = internal_score(p_try)  # uses conv again; but we already had raw and P maybe c? We can compute score of p_try by conv; extra cost.
        # To save, we can use raw maybe not actual p_try. Need score exact. Use internal.
        if sc_try < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc_try; best = p_try.copy()
        p_cur = p_try
    return best, best_sc
```
This calls internal_score each iteration, which does another conv, expensive (extra FFT). We can compute score from p_try using conv? We can reuse raw? p_try is combination of p_cur and pos; its convolution not raw. Need score. Extra conv per iteration doubles cost. Root cheap but still. We can reduce by not scoring every iteration? But need track. Could score every 5 iterations and at end; but may miss. Maybe score every iteration okay: n=7k M=16k, 3-4 FFT per root + 1 internal = maybe 5 transforms. 1000 iterations still seconds. But phases many. We'll score every 10? For root schedule many. Let's improve: Compute c_try using `fast_self_conv(pos?)`? p_try not positive only. We need score. Could use an approximate score from target? But final important. We'll compute every iteration maybe okay. 2400s can handle tens of thousands iterations? 10k*5*0.001=50s. Fine.

But internal_score uses `fast_self_conv(a)` and a sum. If p_try sum1. okay.

`top_clip_target` function:
```
def top_clip_target(c, frac, red):
    L=len(c)
    if frac <=0: return c.copy()
    k=max(1, int(frac*L))
    thr=np.partition(c, L-k)[-k]
    tau=max(thr*(1.0-red), np.mean(c))
    c2=np.minimum(c,tau)
    s=c2.sum()
    if s<=0: return c.copy()
    return c2/s
```
If red were >thr maybe tau. We pass red=strength. Good.

`mirror_smooth_descent` scoring: We compute c already, line search c_try. Use no extra internal except update sc from c_try. We need score of current p: from c. If not updated, compute current c? We have c. Good. In line search, c_try. We can avoid internal_score each trial. At end, if p changed, we have c_trial? We can keep c_cur? Let's implement optimized.

```
def mirror_smooth_descent(p, ks=(1,3,10,30,100), eta0=0.05, eta_max=0.5, max_iter=100000, time_end=None, momentum=0.85, line_trials=4):
    p=sanitize(p); best=p.copy(); best_sc=internal_score(p)
    n=len(p)
    for k in ks:
       beta=float(max(1, k*n))
       eta=eta0; g_buf=np.zeros(n); stalled=0
       for it in range(max_iter):
          if time... break
          c=fast_self_conv(p); L=lse; sc=2n max; update best
          w=softmax_weights(c,beta)
          g=gradient_from_weights(p,w)
          g-=g.mean()
          g_buf=momentum*g_buf+(1-momentum)*g
          rms=...; if rms<... break
          g_use=g_buf/rms
          L0=L
          trial=eta; accepted=False; best_trial=None; best_L=L0; best_sc_t=sc; best_ctry=None
          for _line in range(line_trials):
             u=-trial*g_use; u=clip; p_try; c_try; L_try; sc_try
             if L_try < best_L:
                best_L=L_try; best_p=p_try; best_sc_t=sc_try; best_c=c_try
             if L_try <= L0 + 1e-15:
                accepted=True; p=p_try; c=c_try; L=L_try; sc=sc_try; if sc<best_sc update; break
             trial*=0.5
          if not accepted:
             if best_p is not None and best_L < L0 - 1e-15:
                p=best_p; c=best_c; L=best_L; sc=best_sc_t; update
             else:
                eta *=0.5
                if eta<1e-5: break
                continue
          eta = min(eta_max, eta*1.05)
          # if L decreased tiny, stalled++
          if L0 - L < 1e-14:
             stalled+=1
          else: stalled=0
          if stalled>200: break
    return best,best_sc
```

Use `topk_weights`:
```
def topk_weights(c, limit):
   L=len(c)
   if limit >= L:
      return np.full(L,1.0/L)
   if limit <1: limit=1
   idx=np.argpartition(c, L-limit)[-limit:]
   w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0/limit
   return w
```
This uses full top limit; if limit large e.g. active_frac 0.2 L~14k =>2800, gradient high cost same.

```
def sanitize(a, floor=1e-20):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
       return np.array([1.0,1.0])/2
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S=arr.sum()
    if S < 1e-12:
       return np.ones(arr.size, dtype=float)/arr.size
    arr = np.maximum(arr, floor)
    S=arr.sum()
    if S < 1e-12:
       return np.ones(arr.size, dtype=float)/arr.size
    return arr/S
```
Floor adds n*1e-20; for n large sum maybe 2e-16; still negligible. But if many zeros, floor can make uniform tiny; score changes tiny.

`fast_self_conv`:
```
def fast_self_conv(a):
    n=len(a)
    L=2*n-1
    M=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,M)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,M)[:L]
    # Due to roundoff, tiny negative; but max may be affected? set small negative zero
    c = np.where(c < 0.0, 0.0, c)
    return c
```
Setting all negative to 0 changes sum slightly. Valid because true nonnegative; roundoff only. If due FFT error large? no.

def internal_score(a):
    n=len(a)
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S<1e-12: return float('inf')
    c=fast_self_conv(a)
    M=max
    return 2.0*n*M/(S*S)
```

`lse_from_c` stable:
```
def lse_from_c(c,beta):
    m=float(np.max(c))
    z=beta*(c-m)
    z=np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    s=float(np.sum(np.exp(z)))
    if s <=0: return m
    return m + float(np.log(s))/beta
```
Since z max 0. If beta huge and c has NaN? no.

def gradient_from_weights(p,w):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    if len(w)!=L:
       w=np.asarray(w).ravel(); if len<w: pad; else trim
    p_rev=p[::-1]
    conv_len=L+n-1
    M=1 << (conv_len-1).bit_length()
    Wr=np.fft.rfft(w,M); Pr=np.fft.rfft(p_rev,M)
    cross=np.fft.irfft(Wr*Pr,M)
    return 2.0*cross[n-1:2*n-1]
```

def fienup_amplitude(p, target_kind='peaked', gamma=5.0, alpha=0.5, n_iters=1000, time_end=None):

   if kind == 'flat': target=np.ones; elif 'peaked': target=1/(1+gamma*t*t); elif 'exp': target=np.exp(-gamma*t); else ...
   target = target / float(np.median(target))
   for it:
      A=rfft; amp=abs; amp_safe=max(amp,1e-20); scale=float(np.median(amp)); target_scaled=target*scale
      amp_new=amp_safe*(target_scaled/amp_safe)**alpha
      phase=np.angle(A)
      P_new=amp_new*np.exp(1j*phase)
      P_new[-1]=P_new[-1].real
      raw=irfft(P_new,M)[:n]
      p_try=sanitize(np.maximum(raw,0))
      sc=internal_score; update
   return best
```

### `resample_mass` code correct for upsample
```
def resample_mass(a,n_new):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel(); old=len(a)
    if n_new==old: return sanitize(a)
    if n_new < old:
       idx=np.linspace(0,old,n_new+1).round().astype(int)
       idx[0]=0; idx[-1]=old
       cum=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(a)))
       q=cum[idx[1:]] - cum[idx[:-1]]
    else:
       old_idx=(np.arange(n_new) * old / n_new).astype(int)
       old_idx=np.minimum(old_idx, old-1)
       counts=np.bincount(old_idx, minlength=old)
       counts[counts==0]=1
       q=a[old_idx] / counts[old_idx]
    return sanitize(q)
```
For upsample, if old_idx counts; q sum = sum_j a_j * count_j / count_j = sum a. Good. For downsample, intervals cover, sum preserved. Good.

### Let's write out code mentally and inspect.

```
import numpy as np
import time

def sanitize(a, floor=1e-20):
   arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).ravel()
   if arr.size==0: return np.array([0.5,0.5])
   arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
   arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
   s=float(arr.sum())
   if s<1e-12:
       return np.ones(arr.size,dtype=float)/arr.size
   arr=np.maximum(arr,floor)
   s=float(arr.sum())
   if s<1e-12:
       return np.ones(arr.size,dtype=float)/arr.size
   return arr/s
```
The floor can be too small for large n? no.

`fast_self_conv` returns c sum maybe not exactly 1. If p has floor, sum slightly >? okay.

`internal_score` if `np.max(c)` negative? c max 0, score0? If all zero but S small handled. okay.

`lse_from_c` if beta=0? not.

```python
"""
Step-function coefficient optimizer.

Algorithm summary:
- Work with normalized nonnegative coefficients.
- Use FFT-based exact self-convolution for objective evaluation.
- Primary novel routine: a polynomial square-root target iteration.
  Given a current coefficient vector p, form its square convolution c,
  blend c toward a flatter target c_t, take the DFT F of c_t, and Newton-
  iterate p_fft <- 0.5*(p_fft + F/p_fft) toward a complex square root.
  Project back to nonnegative real length-n support by clipping and
  normalization.  This directly attacks reduced peak coefficients of p^2.
- Secondary polish: mirror-descent on an LSE-smoothed maximum-convolution
  objective, plus a top-peak exact mirror descent.
- Diversifying initializers include one-sided singular priors, central
  binomial edge profiles, a direct flat-target square root, and parent
  variants.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def sanitize(a, floor=1e-20):
    """Convert input to a clean nonnegative float array with sum 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.array([0.5, 0.5], dtype=float)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return np.ones(arr.size, dtype=float) / arr.size
    arr = np.maximum(arr, floor)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return np.ones(arr.size, dtype=float) / arr.size
    return arr / s


def fast_self_conv(a):
    """Exact linear self-convolution via zero-padded FFT."""
    n = len(a)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    m = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, m)[:L]
    c = np.where(c < 0.0, 0.0, c)
    return c


def internal_score(a):
    """Fast internal objective."""
    n = len(a)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    c = fast_self_conv(a)
    return float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s))


def lse_from_c(c, beta):
    """Log-sum-exp smoothing of max(c)."""
    m = float(np.max(c))
    z = beta * (c - m)
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(np.exp(z)))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return m
    return m + float(np.log(s)) / beta


def softmax_weights(c, beta):
    """Stable softmax weights over convolution coefficients."""
    m = float(np.max(c))
    z = beta * (c - m)
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(len(c), 1.0 / len(c), dtype=float)
    return w / s


def gradient_from_weights(p, w):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k w_k c_k with c = p * p.
    This equals 2 * cross-correlation of w with p.
    """
    n = len(p)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if len(w) != L:
        w = np.asarray(w, dtype=float).ravel()
        if w.size < L:
            tmp = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
            tmp[:w.size] = w
            w = tmp
        else:
            w = w[:L]

    p_rev = p[::-1]
    conv_len = L + n - 1
    m = 1 << (conv_len - 1).bit_length()
    Wr = np.fft.rfft(w, m)
    Pr = np.fft.rfft(p_rev, m)
    cross = np.fft.irfft(Wr * Pr, m)
    return 2.0 * cross[n - 1:2 * n - 1]


def topk_weights(c, limit):
    """Uniform weights on the largest `limit` coefficients."""
    L = len(c)
    if limit >= L:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=float)
    if limit < 1:
        limit = 1
    idx = np.argpartition(c, L - limit)[-limit:]
    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    w[idx] = 1.0 / limit
    return w


def top_clip_target(c, frac, red):
    """Mildly clip the top fraction of c, then renormalize."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float).copy()
    L = len(c)
    if frac <= 0.0:
        return c
    k = int(round(frac * L))
    if k <= 1:
        thr = float(np.max(c))
    elif k >= L:
        return c
    else:
        thr = float(np.partition(c, L - k)[-k])
    tau = max(thr * (1.0 - red), float(np.mean(c)))
    c2 = np.minimum(c, tau)
    s = float(c2.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return c
    return c2 / s


def flat_root_candidate(n):
    """Initial coefficient guess from the square root of a flat convolution."""
    L = 2 * n - 1
    m = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    flat = np.ones(L, dtype=float) / L
    F = np.fft.rfft(flat, m)
    P = np.sqrt(F)
    P[-1] = P[-1].real
    raw = np.fft.irfft(P, m)[:n]
    return sanitize(np.maximum(raw, 0.0))


def resample_mass(a, n_new):
    """Resample a probability vector to a new length, preserving total mass."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    old = len(a)
    if n_new == old:
        return sanitize(a)
    if n_new < old:
        idx = np.linspace(0, old, n_new + 1).round().astype(int)
        idx[0] = 0
        idx[-1] = old
        cum = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(a)))
        q = cum[idx[1:]] - cum[idx[:-1]]
    else:
        old_idx = (np.arange(n_new, dtype=float) * old / float(n_new)).astype(int)
        old_idx = np.minimum(old_idx, old - 1)
        counts = np.bincount(old_idx, minlength=old).astype(float)
        counts[counts == 0] = 1.0
        q = a[old_idx] / counts[old_idx]
    return sanitize(q)


def build_structured_candidates(n, rng, parent):
    """Produce diverse nonnegative length-n initializers."""
    cands = []

    # Uniform
    cands.append(np.ones(n, dtype=float) * 0.5)

    # One-sided power laws
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 1.0
    for alpha in (0.45, 0.50, 0.55):
        cands.append(x ** (-alpha))

    left = x ** (-0.5)
    right = left[::-1]
    cands.append(left.copy())
    cands.append(right.copy())
    for eps in (0.1, 0.3, 1.0):
        cands.append(left + eps * right)

    # Central-binomial edge profile
    cb = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    cb[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(n - 1):
        cb[i + 1] = cb[i] * (2 * i + 1) / (2 * i + 2)
    cands.append(cb.copy())
    cands.append(cb[::-1].copy())
    cands.append(cb + cb[::-1])

    # Tapered one-sided
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=float) / max(1, n - 1)
    cands.append(left * (0.7 + 0.3 * np.cos(np.pi * t)))

    # Random low-frequency smooth positive
    base = np.ones(n, dtype=float) * 0.5
    for _ in range(5):
        freq = rng.uniform(1.0, 8.0)
        phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        amp = rng.uniform(0.05, 0.15)
        base += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * freq * t + phase)
    cands.append(np.maximum(base, 0.02))

    # Direct flat-target square root
    cands.append(flat_root_candidate(n))

    # Parent and perturbed parent
    if parent is not None:
        if len(parent) == n:
            parent_n = parent
        else:
            parent_n = resample_mass(parent, n)
        parent_n = sanitize(parent_n)
        cands.append(parent_n)
        for noise in (0.01, 0.03):
            cands.append(parent_n * (1.0 + noise * rng.standard_normal(n)))

    return cands


def mirror_smooth_descent(p,
                          ks=(1, 3, 10, 30, 100),
                          eta0=0.05,
                          eta_max=0.5,
                          max_iter=100000,
                          time_end=None,
                          momentum=0.85,
                          line_trials=4):
    """
    Mirror-descent on LSE-smoothed max convolution.
    Multiplicative updates keep p nonnegative; normalization keeps sum 1.
    """
    p = sanitize(p)
    n = len(p)
    best = p.copy()
    best_sc = internal_score(p)

    for k in ks:
        if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
            break
        p_cur = best.copy()
        beta = float(max(1, k * n))
        eta = eta0
        g_buf = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
        stalled = 0

        for _ in range(max_iter):
            if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
                break

            c = fast_self_conv(p_cur)
            s = float(p_cur.sum())
            sc = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s))
            if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best = p_cur.copy()

            w = softmax_weights(c, beta)
            g = gradient_from_weights(p_cur, w)
            g -= float(np.mean(g))
            g_buf = momentum * g_buf + (1.0 - momentum) * g
            rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(np.square(g_buf))))
            if not np.isfinite(rms) or rms < 1e-15:
                break
            g_use = g_buf / rms

            L0 = lse_from_c(c, beta)
            trial_eta = eta
            accepted = False
            best_p = None
            best_L = L0
            best_sc_t = sc
            best_c = c

            for _line in range(line_trials):
                if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
                    break
                u = -trial_eta * g_use
                u = np.clip(u, -25.0, 25.0)
                p_try = p_cur * np.exp(u)
                if not np.all(np.isfinite(p_try)):
                    trial_eta *= 0.5
                    continue
                st = float(p_try.sum())
                if st < 1e-12:
                    trial_eta *= 0.5
                    continue
                p_try = p_try / st
                c_try = fast_self_conv(p_try)
                L_try = lse_from_c(c_try, beta)
                st2 = float(p_try.sum())
                sc_try = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c_try)) / (st2 * st2))

                if L_try < best_L:
                    best_L = L_try
                    best_p = p_try
                    best_sc_t = sc_try
                    best_c = c_try

                if L_try <= L0 + 1e-15:
                    accepted = True
                    p_cur = p_try
                    c = c_try
                    sc = sc_try
                    if sc < best_sc:
                        best_sc = sc
                        best = p_cur.copy()
                    break

                trial_eta *= 0.5

            if not accepted:
                if best_p is not None and best_L < L0 - 1e-15:
                    p_cur = best_p
                    c = best_c
                    sc = best_sc_t
                    if sc < best_sc:
                        best_sc = sc
                        best = p_cur.copy()
                else:
                    eta *= 0.5
                    if eta < 1e-5:
                        break
                    continue

            eta = min(eta_max, eta * 1.05)
            if L0 - L_try < 1e-14:
                stalled += 1
            else:
                stalled = 0
            if stalled > 200:
                break

    return best, best_sc


def mirror_exact_descent(p,
                         active_fracs=(0.10, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01),
                         eta0=0.04,
                         eta_max=0.35,
                         max_iter=100000,
                         time_end=None,
                         momentum=0.80,
                         line_trials=4):
    """
    Subgradient/mirror descent for the exact maximum coefficient.
    Uses uniform weights on top k coefficients for stability.
    """
    p = sanitize(p)
    n = len(p)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    best = p.copy()
    best_sc = internal_score(p)

    for frac in active_fracs:
        if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
            break
        p_cur = best.copy()
        top_limit = max(20, int(round(frac * L)))
        top_limit = min(top_limit, L)
        eta = eta0
        g_buf = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
        stalled = 0

        for _ in range(max_iter):
            if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
                break

            c = fast_self_conv(p_cur)
            s = float(p_cur.sum())
            sc = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s))
            if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best = p_cur.copy()

            M = float(np.max(c))
            w = topk_weights(c, top_limit)
            g = gradient_from_weights(p_cur, w)
            g -= float(np.mean(g))
            g_buf = momentum * g_buf + (1.0 - momentum) * g
            rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(np.square(g_buf))))
            if not np.isfinite(rms) or rms < 1e-15:
                break
            g_use = g_buf / rms

            trial_eta = eta
            accepted = False
            best_p = None
            best_M = M
            best_sc_t = sc
            best_c = c

            for _line in range(line_trials):
                if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
                    break
                u = -trial_eta * g_use
                u = np.clip(u, -25.0, 25.0)
                p_try = p_cur * np.exp(u)
                if not np.all(np.isfinite(p_try)):
                    trial_eta *= 0.5
                    continue
                st = float(p_try.sum())
                if st < 1e-12:
                    trial_eta *= 0.5
                    continue
                p_try = p_try / st
                c_try = fast_self_conv(p_try)
                st2 = float(p_try.sum())
                sc_try = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c_try)) / (st2 * st2))

                if sc_try < best_sc_t:
                    best_sc_t = sc_try
                    best_p = p_try
                    best_c = c_try
                    best_M = float(np.max(c_try))

                if sc_try <= sc + 1e-15:
                    accepted = True
                    p_cur = p_try
                    c = c_try
                    M = best_M
                    sc = sc_try
                    if sc < best_sc:
                        best_sc = sc
                        best = p_cur.copy()
                    break

                trial_eta *= 0.5

            if not accepted:
                if best_p is not None and best_sc_t < sc - 1e-15:
                    p_cur = best_p
                    c = best_c
                    sc = best_sc_t
                    M = best_M
                    if sc < best_sc:
                        best_sc = sc
                        best = p_cur.copy()
                else:
                    eta *= 0.5
                    if eta < 1e-5:
                        break
                    continue

            eta = min(eta_max, eta * 1.05)
            if sc - best_sc_t < 1e-15:
                stalled += 1
            else:
                stalled = 0
            if stalled > 200:
                break

    return best, best_sc


def root_square_target(p,
                       mode="flat",
                       strength=0.1,
                       top_frac=0.1,
                       proj_blend=0.7,
                       n_iters=1000,
                       time_end=None,
                       newton_steps=2):
    """
    Polynomial square-root target iteration.

    The target c_t is a blend of the current square convolution and a
    flatter target.  In the frequency domain, Newton iterate toward a
    complex square root P such that FFT(c_t) = P^2.  Project P back to
    a nonnegative length-n sequence.
    """
    p = sanitize(p)
    n = len(p)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    m = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    flat = np.ones(L, dtype=float) / L
    flat = flat.copy()

    best = p.copy()
    best_sc = internal_score(p)
    p_cur = p.copy()

    for _ in range(n_iters):
        if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
            break

        A = np.fft.rfft(p_cur, m)
        c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, m)[:L]
        c = np.where(c < 0.0, 0.0, c)
        s = float(p_cur.sum())
        sc = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s)) if s > 1e-12 else float("inf")
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best = p_cur.copy()

        c_for = c.copy()
        csum = float(c_for.sum())
        if csum <= 0.0:
            break
        c_for /= csum

        if mode == "flat":
            c_t = (1.0 - strength) * c_for + strength * flat
        elif mode == "clip":
            c2 = top_clip_target(c_for, top_frac, strength)
            c_t = (1.0 - strength) * c_for + strength * c2
        else:
            c_t = c_for

        c_t = np.maximum(c_t, 0.0)
        ts = float(c_t.sum())
        if ts <= 0.0:
            break
        c_t /= ts

        F = np.fft.rfft(c_t, m)
        P = A.copy()

        maxF = float(np.max(np.abs(F))) if F.size else 1.0
        eps = max(1e-14, 1e-12 * max(1.0, float(np.sqrt(maxF))))

        for _ns in range(newton_steps):
            absP = np.abs(P)
            P_safe = P.copy()
            small = absP <= eps
            if np.any(small):
                P_safe[small] = np.sqrt(F[small])

            absF = np.abs(F)
            valid = absF > (eps * eps)
            P_new = P.copy()

            if np.any(valid):
                with np.errstate(divide="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
                    P_new[valid] = 0.5 * (P_safe[valid] + F[valid] / P_safe[valid])
            if np.any(~valid):
                P_new[~valid] = 0.5 * P_safe[~valid]

            P_new = np.where(np.isfinite(P_new), P_new, P)
            P = P_new
            P[-1] = P[-1].real

        raw = np.fft.irfft(P, m)[:n]
        pos = np.maximum(raw, 0.0)
        sp = float(pos.sum())
        if sp < 1e-12:
            continue
        pos = pos / sp

        if proj_blend >= 1.0:
            p_try = pos
        elif proj_blend <= 0.0:
            p_try = pos
        else:
            p_try = (1.0 - proj_blend) * p_cur + proj_blend * pos

        p_try = sanitize(p_try)
        sc_try = internal_score(p_try)
        if sc_try < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc_try
            best = p_try.copy()

        p_cur = p_try

    return best, best_sc


def fienup_amplitude(p,
                     target_kind="peaked",
                     gamma=4.0,
                     alpha=0.5,
                     n_iters=1000,
                     time_end=None):
    """
    Amplitude-shaping polish in the Fourier domain.
    Preserves current phase of P and moves amplitudes toward a target shape.
    """
    p = sanitize(p)
    n = len(p)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    m = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    best = p.copy()
    best_sc = internal_score(p)

    nfreq = m // 2 + 1
    freq = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, nfreq)
    if target_kind == "flat":
        target = np.ones(nfreq, dtype=float)
    elif target_kind == "peaked":
        target = 1.0 / (1.0 + gamma * freq * freq)
    elif target_kind == "exp":
        target = np.exp(-gamma * freq)
    else:
        target = 1.0 / (1.0 + max(1e-6, gamma) * freq)

    tmed = float(np.median(target))
    if tmed <= 0.0:
        tmed = 1.0
    target = target / tmed

    for _ in range(n_iters):
        if time_end is not None and time.monotonic() > time_end:
            break

        A = np.fft.rfft(p, m)
        amp = np.abs(A)
        amp_safe = np.maximum(amp, 1e-20)
        med = float(np.median(amp))
        if med <= 0.0:
            break
        target_scaled = target * med
        amp_new = amp_safe * (target_scaled / amp_safe) ** alpha
        ratio = np.clip(amp_new / amp_safe, 1e-8, 1e8)
        A_new = A * ratio
        A_new[-1] = A_new[-1].real

        raw = np.fft.irfft(A_new, m)[:n]
        p_try = sanitize(np.maximum(raw, 0.0))
        sc_try = internal_score(p_try)
        if sc_try < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc_try
            best = p_try.copy()
        p = p_try

    return best, best_sc


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """
    Main search entry point.  Uses a multi-stage strategy:
    1. Build diverse candidates and select the best.
    2. Square-root target iteration from small to larger target flatness.
    3. Try the best competitive candidate variants.
    4. Smoothed mirror descent and exact top-peak mirror descent.
    5. Final targeted polish and small restarts.
    """
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = float(budget_s)
    usable = max(120.0, total_budget - 60.0)
    t_abort = t_start + usable

    # Load parent if available.
    parent = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float).ravel()
        if arr.size >= 2:
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if arr.sum() > 1e-8:
                parent = sanitize(arr)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    MAX_LEN = 16384
    if parent is not None and len(parent) > MAX_LEN:
        parent = resample_mass(parent, MAX_LEN)

    if parent is not None:
        n_main = len(parent)
        best = parent.copy()
    else:
        n_main = 8192
        best = np.ones(n_main, dtype=float) / n_main

    best_score = internal_score(best)
    print(f"Initial length: {n_main}, parent present: {parent is not None}")
    print(f"Initial internal score: {best_score:.10f}")

    # Generate candidates.
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    raw_cands = build_structured_candidates(n_main, rng, parent)

    entries = []
    for idx, c in enumerate(raw_cands):
        c_n = sanitize(c)
        if len(c_n) != n_main:
            continue
        sc = internal_score(c_n)
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = c_n.copy()
            print(f"  Better candidate {idx}: {sc:.10f}")
        entries.append((sc, idx, c_n))

    entries.sort()
    top_cands = []
    for _, _, c in entries[:6]:
        top_cands.append(c)

    # Stage A: square-root target on current best, from gentle to stronger.
    print("\nStage A: polynomial square-root target iteration")
    # (strength, top_frac, proj_blend, end_fraction)
    flat_stages = (
        (0.008, 0.00, 0.90, 0.04),
        (0.018, 0.00, 0.80, 0.09),
        (0.040, 0.00, 0.70, 0.15),
        (0.090, 0.00, 0.60, 0.22),
        (0.180, 0.00, 0.50, 0.30),
    )
    for strength, top_frac, pblend, end_frac in flat_stages:
        end_t = t_start + end_frac * usable
        if time.monotonic() >= end_t:
            continue
        b, sc = root_square_target(
            best,
            mode="flat",
            strength=strength,
            top_frac=top_frac,
            proj_blend=pblend,
            n_iters=100000,
            time_end=end_t,
            newton_steps=2,
        )
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = b
            print(f"  Root flat {strength:.3f}: {sc:.10f}")

    # Mild clipping targets can help avoid overflat infeasible targets.
    end_clip = t_start + 0.38 * usable
    if time.monotonic() < end_clip:
        for strength, top_frac, pblend in (
            (0.10, 0.10, 0.70),
            (0.20, 0.20, 0.60),
        ):
            if time.monotonic() >= end_clip:
                break
            b, sc = root_square_target(
                best,
                mode="clip",
                strength=strength,
                top_frac=top_frac,
                proj_blend=pblend,
                n_iters=100000,
                time_end=end_clip,
                newton_steps=2,
            )
            if sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best = b
                print(f"  Root clip s={strength:.2f} frac={top_frac:.2f}: {sc:.10f}")

    # Stage B: apply root target to strong alternative candidates.
    end_alt = t_start + 0.48 * usable
    print("\nStage B: root iterations on alternative candidates")
    for c in top_cands[1:5]:
        if time.monotonic() >= end_alt:
            break
        b, sc = root_square_target(
            c,
            mode="flat",
            strength=0.12,
            top_frac=0.0,
            proj_blend=0.70,
            n_iters=100000,
            time_end=end_alt,
            newton_steps=2,
        )
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = b
            print(f"  Root alternative: {sc:.10f}")

    # Stage C: smooth LSE mirror descent.
    end_smooth = t_start + 0.74 * usable
    print("\nStage C: LSE mirror-descent")
    b, sc = mirror_smooth_descent(
        best,
        ks=(1, 3, 10, 30, 100),
        eta0=0.05,
        eta_max=0.5,
        max_iter=100000,
        time_end=end_smooth,
        momentum=0.85,
        line_trials=4,
    )
    if sc < best_score:
        best_score = sc
        best = b
        print(f"  LSE mirror improved: {sc:.10f}")

    # Stage D: exact top-peak mirror descent.
    end_exact = t_start + 0.90 * usable
    print("\nStage D: exact top-peak mirror-descent")
    b, sc = mirror_exact_descent(
        best,
        active_fracs=(0.10, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01),
        eta0=0.04,
        eta_max=0.35,
        max_iter=100000,
        time_end=end_exact,
        momentum=0.80,
        line_trials=4,
    )
    if sc < best_score:
        best_score = sc
        best = b
        print(f"  Exact mirror improved: {sc:.10f}")

    # Stage E: final targeted polish.
    print("\nStage E: final polish")
    end_root_final = min(t_abort - 60.0, t_start + 0.95 * usable)
    if time.monotonic() < end_root_final:
        b, sc = root_square_target(
            best,
            mode="flat",
            strength=0.03,
            top_frac=0.0,
            proj_blend=0.60,
            n_iters=100000,
            time_end=end_root_final,
            newton_steps=2,
        )
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = b
            print(f"  Final root: {sc:.10f}")

    end_fienup = min(t_abort - 30.0, t_start + 0.98 * usable)
    if time.monotonic() < end_fienup:
        b, sc = fienup_amplitude(
            best,
            target_kind="peaked",
            gamma=3.0,
            alpha=0.45,
            n_iters=100000,
            time_end=end_fienup,
        )
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = b
            print(f"  Fienup peaked: {sc:.10f}")

    if time.monotonic() < t_abort - 15.0:
        b, sc = mirror_exact_descent(
            best,
            active_fracs=(0.05, 0.02),
            eta0=0.025,
            eta_max=0.25,
            max_iter=100000,
            time_end=t_abort,
            momentum=0.80,
            line_trials=3,
        )
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = b
            print(f"  Final exact mirror: {sc:.10f}")

    # Small deterministic restarts.
    if time.monotonic() < t_abort - 10.0:
        for noise in (0.008, 0.02):
            if time.monotonic() >= t_abort - 10.0:
                break
            p_noise = best * (1.0 + noise * rng.standard_normal(len(best)))
            p_noise = sanitize(p_noise)
            b, sc = mirror_exact_descent(
                p_noise,
                active_fracs=(0.05,),
                eta0=0.03,
                eta_max=0.25,
                max_iter=2000,
                time_end=t_abort,
                momentum=0.75,
                line_trials=3,
            )
            if sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best = b
                print(f"  Restart noise={noise:.3f}: {sc:.10f}")

    # Final valid output.
    best = sanitize(best)
    if len(best) > MAX_LEN:
        best = resample_mass(best, MAX_LEN)

    out = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in best]
    if sum(out) < 0.01:
        out = [0.5] * 64

    try:
        verified = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
    except Exception:
        verified = internal_score(out)

    elapsed = time.monotonic() - t_start
    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
    print(f"FINAL internal score : {internal_score(out):.12f}")
    print(f"FINAL verified score : {verified:.12f}")
    print(f"Length               : {len(out)}")
    print(f"Time used            : {elapsed:.1f}s / {total_budget}s")
    print("=" * 70)

    return out
```
